title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Vittorio Veneto">Diocese of Vittorio Veneto</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Venice" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Venice">Patriarchate of Venice</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Catechists" class="mw-redirect" title="Catechists">Catechists</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Diocesan_process">Diocesan process</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Diocesan process">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The process for the canonisation for John Paul I formally began in 1990 with the petition by 226 <a href="/wiki/Brazilian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Brazilian people">Brazilian</a> bishops, including four cardinals. The petition was addressed directly to Pope John Paul II.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 26 August 2002, Bishop Vincenzo Savio announced the start of the preliminary phase to collect documents and testimonies necessary to start the process of <a href="/wiki/Canonisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonisation">canonisation</a>. On 8 June 2003 the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> gave its assent to the work and on 17 June transferred the forum for the beatification process from Rome to Belluno-Feltre while also declaring the late pope as a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a> after declaring "<a href="/wiki/Nihil_obstat" title="Nihil obstat">nihil obstat</a>" (no objections to the cause). On 23 November, on the <a href="/wiki/Feast_of_Christ_the_King" title="Feast of Christ the King">Feast of Christ the King</a>, the diocesan process formally opened in the Cathedral Basilica of <a href="/wiki/Belluno" title="Belluno">Belluno</a> with <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a> in charge and presiding over the inauguration.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The diocesan inquiry for the cause subsequently concluded on 11 November 2006 in <a href="/wiki/Belluno" title="Belluno">Belluno</a> with all the evidence collected being sent to the C.C.S. which received their validation on 13 June 2008. On 13 June 2008, the Vatican began the "Roman" phase of the beatification process for John Paul I, in which they would assess the documents and witness testimonies collected during the diocesan inquiry.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Roman_phase">Roman phase</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Roman phase">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The documents in regard to the cause were supposed to be delivered to the prefect of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints, <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Amato" title="Angelo Amato">Angelo Amato</a> on 17 October 2012 (the one-hundredth anniversary of the late pope's birth), in a large <a href="/wiki/Positio" title="Positio">Positio</a> dossier (consisting of a biography and investigation into his virtues) to examine the pros and cons of the cause. This was delayed when the cause's supporters wanted another check over all the documents. In a mass at Belluno on 20 July 2014, <a href="/wiki/Tarcisio_Bertone" title="Tarcisio Bertone">Tarcisio Bertone</a> announced that the cause of beatification was set to advance. The cardinal highlighted that the Positio would be delivered in September 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> But the dossier was not submitted to the C.C.S. until 17 October 2016; there were five volumes with around 3600 pages in total.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 27 August 2015, Bishop Giuseppe Andrich announced that John Paul I would be beatified "soon". In a homily delivered during Mass in Canale d'Agordo, Luciani's home town, on the 37th anniversary of his election as Pope, Andrich said Church authorities had concluded the investigation into Luciani's heroic virtues. Following the conclusion of the writing of the "Positio" dossier (3652 pages in total), they received several messages affirming personal experience of Luciani's holiness, including a handwritten card from <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Emeritus Benedict XVI</a>. The testimony of a Pope or former Pope in considering a candidate for sainthood is extremely unusual. Benedict XVI apparently recommended waiving the requirement for miracles in Luciani's case.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>To determine whether or not the late pontiff should be declared <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a>, theologians and the members of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints must determine if the late pope lived a life of <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtue</a>. This meeting took place on 1 June 2017 in which theologians unanimously approved the fact that the late pope exercised virtues to a heroic degree.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> The cardinal and bishop members discussed the cause on 7 November 2017 and issued their unanimous approval.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> named John Paul I as <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> on 8 November 2017 after confirming his <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtue</a> per the <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_virtues" title="Cardinal virtues">cardinal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Theological_virtues" title="Theological virtues">theological virtues</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Herald_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Herald-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification">Beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1134653256">.mw-parser-output .side-box{margin:4px 0;box-sizing:border-box;border:1px solid #aaa;font-size:88%;line-height:1.25em;background-color:#f9f9f9;display:flow-root}.mw-parser-output .side-box-abovebelow,.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{padding:0.25em 0.9em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-image{padding:2px 0 2px 0.9em;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-imageright{padding:2px 0.9em 2px 0;text-align:center}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .side-box-flex{display:flex;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .side-box-text{flex:1}}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .side-box{width:238px}.mw-parser-output .side-box-right{clear:right;float:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .side-box-left{margin-right:1em}}</style><div class="side-box side-box-right plainlinks sistersitebox"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409">
<div class="side-box-flex">
<div class="side-box-image"><span class="noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/40px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="22" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/60px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Wikinews-logo.svg/80px-Wikinews-logo.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="759" data-file-height="415" /></span></span></div>
<div class="side-box-text plainlist">Wikinews has related news:
<div><ul><li><a href="https://en.wikinews.org/wiki/Pope_Francis_beatifies_short-lived,_%27smiling_Pope%27_John_Paul_I" class="extiw" title="wikinews:Pope Francis beatifies short-lived, &#39;smiling Pope&#39; John Paul I"> Pope Francis beatifies short-lived, 'smiling Pope' John Paul I</a></li></ul></div></div></div>
</div>
<p>For Luciani to be <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a>, the investigators had to certify at least one <a href="/wiki/Miracle" title="Miracle">miracle</a> attributed to his intercession. For <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonisation</a> there must be a second miracle, though the reigning pope may waive these requirements altogether, as is often done in the case of beatified popes.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It was reported in 2016 that a potential miracle attributed to the late pontiff's intercession happened to a nun in <a href="/wiki/Buenos_Aires" title="Buenos Aires">Buenos Aires</a> in <a href="/wiki/Argentina" title="Argentina">Argentina</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The vice-postulator for the cause, Stefania Falasca, reported in a piece for <i><a href="/wiki/Avvenire" title="Avvenire">Avvenire</a></i> that medical consultants in Rome deemed the healing of the Argentine nun as a miracle on 31 October 2019 as there was no possible scientific or medical explanation. Theologians likewise provided their approval on 6 May 2021 after determining that the healing came as a direct result of the late pope's intercession. Falasca reported that the cardinal and bishop members of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints were scheduled to meet to discuss the cause in October, thereby implying that a 2022 beatification was likely.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Francis authorized a decree that recognized the miracle on 13 October 2021; it enabled for John Paul I to be beatified at Saint Peter's Square on 4 September 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Miracle">Miracle</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Miracle">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Following the announcement that John Paul I would be beatified, details were released that the miracle in question was the recovery of an 11-year-old in Buenos Aires from <a href="/wiki/Encephalitis" title="Encephalitis">inflammatory encephalopathy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Failed_miracle">Failed miracle</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Failed miracle">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The postulation also drew upon the testimony of Giuseppe Denora di Altamura who claimed to have been cured of cancer by the intercession of the late pontiff. An official investigation into the alleged miracle commenced on 14 May 2007 and concluded on 30 May 2009 with the C.C.S. validating the process on 25 March 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The supposed miracle attributed to his intercession was taken to a medical board in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> on 24 April 2015 and the commission came to the conclusion that it was not a miracle that could be attributed to Luciani. This means that another miracle will need to be found before the cause can continue.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Postulation">Postulation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Postulation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The postulator for the cause was Bishop <a href="/wiki/Enrico_dal_Covolo" title="Enrico dal Covolo">Enrico dal Covolo</a> from 2003 until 2016 when <a href="/wiki/Beniamino_Stella" title="Beniamino Stella">Beniamino Stella</a> was appointed to that position. Stefania Falasca is the current vice-postulator.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Titles.2C_styles_and_arms"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Titles,_styles_and_arms">Titles, styles and arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Titles, styles and arms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Titles_and_styles">Titles and styles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Titles and styles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>26 August 1978 – 28 September 1978: <i><a href="/wiki/Holiness_(style)" title="Holiness (style)">His Holiness</a></i> Pope John Paul I</li>
<li>5 March 1973 – 26 August 1978: <i><a href="/wiki/His_Beatitude" class="mw-redirect" title="His Beatitude">His Beatitude</a></i><sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Patriarch Albino Cardinal Luciani</li>
<li>15 December 1969 – 5 March 1973: <i><a href="/wiki/His_Beatitude" class="mw-redirect" title="His Beatitude">His Beatitude</a></i> Patriarch Albino<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>15 December 1958 – 15 December 1969: <i><a href="/wiki/The_Most_Reverend" title="The Most Reverend">The Most Reverend</a></i> Albino Luciani, Bishop of Vittorio Veneto<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arms">Arms</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Arms">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Pope John Paul I was the first pope to abandon the <a href="/wiki/Papal_coronation" title="Papal coronation">coronation</a>, and he was also the first pope to choose a double name (John Paul) for his <a href="/wiki/Papal_name" title="Papal name">papal name</a>. His successor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Karol Józef Wojtyła</a>, chose the same name. He was the first pope to have a Papal Inauguration and the last pope to use the <i><a href="/wiki/Sedia_Gestatoria" class="mw-redirect" title="Sedia Gestatoria">Sedia Gestatoria</a></i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Views_of_successors">Views of successors</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Views of successors">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="John_Paul_II">John Paul II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: John Paul II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg/215px-Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg" decoding="async" width="215" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg/323px-Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg/430px-Museo_papa_Luciani.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a><figcaption>The Pope Luciani museum</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Karol Wojtyła</a> was <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave,_October_1978" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal conclave, October 1978">elected</a> John Paul I's successor as <a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a> on Monday, 16 October 1978. The next day he celebrated <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a> together with the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Sistine_Chapel" title="Sistine Chapel">Sistine Chapel</a>. After the Mass, he delivered his first <i><a href="/wiki/Urbi_et_Orbi" title="Urbi et Orbi">Urbi et Orbi</a></i> (a traditional blessing) message, broadcast worldwide via radio. In it he pledged fidelity to the <a href="/wiki/Second_Vatican_Council" title="Second Vatican Council">Second Vatican Council</a> and paid tribute to his predecessor:<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>What can we say of John Paul I? It seems to us that only yesterday he emerged from this assembly of ours to put on the papal robes—not a light weight. But what warmth of charity, nay, what "an abundant outpouring of love"—which came forth from him in the few days of his ministry and which in his last Sunday address before the <a href="/wiki/Angelus" title="Angelus">Angelus</a> he desired should come upon the world. This is also confirmed by his wise instructions to the faithful who were present at his public audiences on faith, hope and love.</p></blockquote>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Benedict_XVI">Benedict XVI</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Benedict XVI">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Benedict XVI spoke of the late pontiff on 28 September 2008 (the 30th anniversary of John Paul I's death) during his weekly Angelus address. Of the late pope, he said:<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Because of this virtue of his, it only took 33 days for Pope Luciani to win people's hearts. In his addresses he always referred to events in practical life, from his family memories and from popular wisdom. His simplicity was a vehicle for a solid, rich teaching which, thanks to the gift of an exceptional memory and a vast knowledge, he embellished with numerous citations from ecclesiastical and secular writers. Thus, he was an incomparable catechist, following in the footsteps of St <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_X" title="Pope Pius X">Pius X</a>, who came from the same region and was his predecessor first on the throne of St Mark and then on that of St Peter. 'We must feel small before God,' he said during the same Audience. And he added, 'I am not ashamed to feel like a child before his mother; one believes in one's mother; I believe in the Lord, in what he has revealed to me.' These words reveal the full depth of his faith. As we thank God for having given him to the church and to the world, let us treasure his example, striving to cultivate his same humility which enabled him to talk to everyone, especially the small and the 'distant.' For this, let us invoke Mary Most Holy, the humble handmaid of the Lord.</p></blockquote>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Francis">Francis</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Francis">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pope Francis spoke of his predecessor in his 2016 book <i>The Name of God Is Mercy</i> in which Francis recalls how touched he was by his predecessor's writings. More than any of his predecessors mentioned in his book, Francis refers to Luciani the most. The pope referred to Luciani's remarks at the latter's general audience of 6 September 1978 and mentioned how profound that his words were upon him; of the remarks Luciani made, he said:<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>There is the homily when Albino Luciani said he had been chosen because the Lord preferred that certain things not be engraved in bronze or marble but in the dust, so that if the writing had remained, it would have been clear that the merit was only God's.</p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_John_Paul_I&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>In 2006, the Italian Public Broadcasting Service, <a href="/wiki/RAI" title="RAI">RAI</a>, produced a television miniseries about the life of John Paul I, called <i><a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_I:_The_Smile_of_God" title="Pope John Paul I: The Smile of God">Papa Luciani: Il sorriso di Dio</a></i> (literally, "Pope Luciani: The Smile of God"). It stars Italian comedian <a href="/wiki/Neri_Marcor%C3%A8" title="Neri Marcorè">Neri Marcorè</a> in the titular role.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>A conspiracy theory about the Pope's death is portrayed in the 1990 crime film, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Godfather_Part_III" title="The Godfather Part III">The Godfather Part III</a></i>, where he is killed with poison tea in relation to the Vatican Bank.</li></ul>

<li id="cite_note-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-12">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFYallop,_David1985" class="citation book cs1">Yallop, David (1985). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=xQYuAAAAYAAJ"><i>In God's name: an investigation into the murder of Pope John Paul I</i></a>. Corgi. p.&#160;16. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/9780552126403" title="Special:BookSources/9780552126403"><bdi>9780552126403</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=In+God%27s+name%3A+an+investigation+into+the+murder+of+Pope+John+Paul+I&amp;rft.pages=16&amp;rft.pub=Corgi&amp;rft.date=1985&amp;rft.isbn=9780552126403&amp;rft.au=Yallop%2C+David&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3DxQYuAAAAYAAJ&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+John+Paul+I" class="Z3988"></span><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>So strongly did the writings of Couwase [Jean Pierre de Caussade] influence him that Luciani began to think very seriously of becoming a Jesuit. He watched as the first one, then a second, of his close friends went to the rector, Bishop Giouse Cattarossi, and asked for permission to join the Jesuit order. In both instances, permission was granted to them. Luciani would soon decide, so he went and asked for permission. The bishop considered the request, then responded, "No, three is one too many. You had better stay here.</p></blockquote></span>

<li id="cite_note-48"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-48">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite id="CITEREFSeabeckSeabeck2004" class="citation book cs1">Seabeck, Raymond; Seabeck, Lauretta (2004). <i>The Smiling Pope, The Life &amp; Teaching of John Paul I</i>. <q>We must not be deceived by his smile. He listened, he asked for information, he studied. But once he made a decision, he did not go back on it, unless new facts came to light.... With absolute respect to persons, the Pope had no intentions of deviating from what had been the rule of his life and the direction of his pastoral action: fatherly, yes, but absolutely firm in the guidance of the souls entrusted by God to his care.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=The+Smiling+Pope%2C+The+Life+%26+Teaching+of+John+Paul+I&amp;rft.date=2004&amp;rft.aulast=Seabeck&amp;rft.aufirst=Raymond&amp;rft.au=Seabeck%2C+Lauretta&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3APope+John+Paul+I" class="Z3988"></span> <span class="cs1-visible-error citation-comment"><code class="cs1-code">{{<a href="/wiki/Template:Cite_book" title="Template:Cite book">cite book</a>}}</code>: </span><span class="cs1-visible-error citation-comment"><code class="cs1-code">&#124;work=</code> ignored (<a href="/wiki/Help:CS1_errors#periodical_ignored" title="Help:CS1 errors">help</a>)</span></span>
